=====================================================================
1.  WHAT I ASKED FOR — AND THE ONE CORRECTION YOU MUST MAKE FOR ME
=====================================================================

I asked for three things:
  (a) ideas and businesses to make more money,
  (b) make Odin's paper trading run better and make him smarter,
  (c) get "the one percentage" — the win rate — to at least 80% in the
      next two to three weeks.

Do (a) and (b) completely. On (c), here is the correction, and you will
build it this way even though it is not the exact words I used, because
the exact words would make Odin worse:

WIN RATE ON ITS OWN IS THE EASIEST NUMBER IN TRADING TO FAKE.
Any strategy can show 80% or 90% wins by taking tiny profits and letting
losers run. Nine wins of $100 and one loss of $1,000 is a 90% win rate and
a losing system. The number that says whether a system makes money is
EXPECTANCY — (win% × average win) − (loss% × average loss) — together
with PROFIT FACTOR, MAX DRAWDOWN and how many trades the numbers rest on.
A 30% win rate can be excellent and a 70% one a disaster; only the pair
tells you which. Odin already knows this; it is written into his brief.

So the target is NOT "80% win rate at any cost". The target is:

  A SCORECARD (section 6) THAT ODIN MUST BEAT, IN WHICH WIN RATE IS ONE
  OF SEVEN NUMBERS, AND ONE LANE OF STRATEGIES — DEFINED-RISK SHORT
  PREMIUM (section 8) — WHERE 75–85% WIN RATES ARE NORMAL AND HONEST
  BECAUSE THE LOSSES ARE CAPPED BY CONSTRUCTION, NOT BY HOPE.

That lane is the only place an 80% figure means anything. You will build
it, measure it properly, and Odin will report it next to expectancy every
single time so the number can never lie to me.

And on "two to three weeks": you will build, backtest, walk-forward and
put on paper everything in this brief inside 21 days (section 17). What
you will NOT do is claim a strategy is proven in 21 days of paper. The
graduation gates (section 6.4) are counted in trades and in trading days,
and the report says plainly which gate each strategy has passed. A number
from twelve trades is labelled "twelve trades". That honesty is the
product.


=====================================================================
2.  WHERE ODIN IS TODAY, IN ONE LOOK
=====================================================================

Strong already:  the safety shape (paper only, gates, halt), the write
                 discipline, the five-trader structure, the honest
                 reporting, separate realized/unrealized, the id rule.
Weak today:      fills are simulated by our own code against delayed
                 candles (Twelve Data's free tier is not intraday-fresh);
                 three strategies with fixed parameters and no evidence
                 they were ever the right parameters; no regime awareness
                 (the same rules run in a crash and in a melt-up); no
                 walk-forward testing; no slippage/fee model or a naive
                 one; the backtest only sees whatever candles happen to be
                 cached; no options; the councillors do not learn; win
                 rate is tracked but nothing is built around expectancy.
The gap:         Odin has a good cage and a weak animal in it. This brief
                 keeps the cage exactly as it is and replaces the animal.


=====================================================================
3.  THE NEW SHAPE — THREE PLACES, ONE TRUTH
=====================================================================

  THE HALL       the Cloudflare Worker. Runs the live paper tick, holds
                 the portfolio, talks to the paper broker, answers Odin's
                 questions, sends reports. Stays small and cheap. Its job
                 is to EXECUTE decisions, not to discover them.

  THE FORGE      a Python program on my Windows PC (C:\Asgard\forge\).
                 Pulls history, runs backtests, walk-forward, Monte Carlo,
                 parameter-stability tests, the nightly tournament, and
                 writes ONE signed file of verdicts the Hall reads. Runs
                 as a scheduled task at 02:00 Pacific every night and on
                 demand. Its job is to DISCOVER and to DISQUALIFY.

  THE LEDGER     one KV namespace of truth per trader (existing keys),
                 plus one daily equity sample, plus the Forge's verdicts.
                 Everything Odin says comes from the Ledger. If it is not
                 in the Ledger, Odin does not say it.

Rule: the Hall never runs a strategy the Forge has not passed. The Forge
never places an order. A strategy moves from the Forge to the Hall by a
promotion the Forge writes and the Hall reads (section 12.7). There is no
other path.


=====================================================================
4.  DATA — EVERY SOURCE, EVERY LIMIT, AND THE CALL BUDGET
=====================================================================

Verified as of September 2026. Re-verify each on first use and record the
observed limit in docs/DATA.md.

4.1  Alpaca (paper account + data). Free. Sign up once (NEXT-STEPS).
     * Paper accounts are free, created from the dashboard, start at a
       $100k default balance — you will create ours and immediately size
       every trader to the existing $10,000 by rule, not by balance.
     * Market data for paper accounts is the IEX feed only, real-time,
       not the consolidated SIP. Prices can differ from "the market" by
       5–50 basis points on liquid names, much more on illiquid ones.
       We only trade liquid ETFs and large caps, so this is acceptable
       and it is DISCLOSED in the report as "IEX feed".
     * Paper fills: an order fills when it becomes marketable at NBBO;
       10% of fills are randomly PARTIAL; quantity is NOT checked against
       real liquidity. Paper crypto orders hit REAL exchange order books,
       so crypto paper results are more realistic than stock paper.
     * PDT (pattern day trader) rules APPLY on paper: 4+ day trades in 5
       business days with under $25k equity locks the account. Our
       traders do not day trade equities; the risk engine enforces it
       (section 10.4).
     * Options: multi-leg (Level 3) is available in paper. This is what
       makes section 8 possible.
     * Buying power lags fills by ~1 s; never fire two orders back to
       back without reading the account.
     * Timestamps for market hours come back timezone-naive Eastern.
       Convert explicitly, once, in one function.
     Use Alpaca for: paper execution of SPY, QQQ, GLD, USO (and any other
     liquid ETF), crypto pairs it lists, and the options lane.

4.2  Twelve Data. Free, 800 calls/day, intraday on the free tier is NOT
     fresh (treat anything under daily as delayed). Keep it as the
     SECOND source for daily candles and as the fallback when Alpaca is
     unreachable. Budget: at most 120 calls/day, counted in KV.

4.3  Kraken public OHLC. Free, no key, works from Workers. Keep for BTC
     and ETH candles and as a sanity check against Alpaca crypto.

4.4  FRED (free key). CPI, unemployment, fed funds, 10y yield, VIX
     (VIXCLS). Pull once a day after the close. Feeds the regime engine.

4.5  SEC EDGAR (no key; `User-Agent: Name email@domain` header is
     mandatory; 10 requests/second). Full-text search with forms=4 is a
     free insider-buying feed. Used ONLY by the research lane in
     section 12.8 for context lines, never as a trading signal in this
     sprint.

4.6  Treasury FiscalData (no key), Financial Modeling Prep (250/day free,
     earnings calendar). Earnings dates feed the event filter (9.5).

4.7  Do NOT use Alpha Vantage (25/day, effectively dead) or Yahoo
     Finance's unofficial endpoints (unreliable, break without notice).
     Massive (formerly Polygon) free tier is end-of-day and 5 calls/min —
     not worth the wiring.

4.8  History for the Forge: pull daily bars back to 2010 and 1-minute
     bars back as far as Alpaca's free history allows for every symbol
     in the universe (section 7.0), once, into Parquet files on the PC.
     Refresh nightly, incrementally. Never re-download what you have.

4.9  THE CALL BUDGET, enforced in code:
     * Every external call increments a per-source counter in KV, one
       key per source per UTC day, written at most once per minute
       (batch the increments in memory inside a tick).
     * Each source has a hard daily ceiling in config. At 90% the source
       is marked "conserve" and only essential calls run. At 100% it is
       off until midnight UTC and the report says so.
     * The Hall makes at most: one quote batch per open symbol per tick
       (5-minute tick → 78 ticks per session), one account read per tick,
       one order-status read per order. Everything else is nightly.
     * KV writes: the portfolio is written ONLY when something changed —
       a position opened, closed, or a stop moved. One equity sample per
       trader at the close. One journal line per trader per day. One
       report marker per day. Count them: under 60 writes a day in total
       on a busy day. Print the day's count in the close report.


=====================================================================
5.  THE PAPER BROKER — REAL-GRADE FILLS, SAME CAGE
=====================================================================

5.1  Keep src/lib/broker.js's interface exactly (getQuote, getPositions,
     getBalance, placeOrder, cancelOrder, closePosition). Add a second
     PAPER implementation, AlpacaPaperBroker, behind the same interface.
     The existing KV-simulated PaperBroker stays as SimBroker — it is
     the deterministic engine the Forge uses for backtests and the
     fallback when Alpaca is unreachable. LiveBroker remains the stub
     that throws. Nothing else changes shape.

5.2  Selection: config:trading:broker = "alpaca-paper" | "sim". Default
     "alpaca-paper" once the key exists, "sim" until then. Odin's report
     names which one is running. Never both for the same trader.

5.3  Every order carries {agentId, symbol, side, qty, type, stop,
     clientOrderId} where clientOrderId = agentId + ":" + strategy + ":"
     + a ULID. Idempotent: re-sending the same clientOrderId never
     double-fills.

5.4  Reconcile every tick: positions the broker reports vs positions the
     Ledger believes. Any mismatch → halt that trader, log a RECONCILE
     line, include it in the report. Never "fix" a mismatch by writing
     the broker's view over the Ledger silently.

5.5  The sim's fill model, upgraded so backtests are not fairy tales:
     * fill price = next bar's open (never the signal bar's close)
     * slippage = half the estimated spread + a volatility term:
       spread_bps by symbol class (ETF 1 bp, large cap 2 bp, crypto 5 bp,
       options: use the quoted bid/ask, fill at mid minus 15% of width
       for credit, plus for debit) + 0.05 × ATR(14)/price
     * commission: $0 stocks/ETFs, $0.65/contract options, 0.25% crypto
     * 10% of fills partial (random size), matching Alpaca paper
     * stop orders fill at the stop price minus slippage, or at the
       bar's open if the bar gapped through the stop
     * no fills on halted symbols, no fills outside the session, US
       market holidays from a static list for 2026 and 2027 that the
       Sunday report warns about when the last year equals this year.

5.6  Odin's readiness tool stays documentation-only and adds two lines:
     which broker implementation is active, and "no live path exists".


=====================================================================
6.  THE SCORECARD — THE SEVEN NUMBERS, AND THE GATES
=====================================================================

6.1  Per trader, per strategy, and for the whole council, always:
     1. Trades          closed trades in the window (the denominator)
     2. Win rate        wins ÷ trades
     3. Avg win / Avg loss  in $ and as a ratio (payoff ratio)
     4. Expectancy      (win% × avg win) − (loss% × avg loss), in $ per
                        trade and in R (multiples of initial risk)
     5. Profit factor   gross wins ÷ gross losses
     6. Max drawdown    (peak − trough) ÷ peak, on the equity curve
     7. Sharpe (daily)  mean daily return ÷ stdev, × √252, and Sortino
     plus: exposure (% of days in a position), average hold time,
     largest single loss, current losing streak, longest streak ever,
     realized vs unrealized (always separate), fees paid.

6.2  Every number carries its denominator. "Win rate 81% (n=13)" is the
     only allowed way to print a win rate. A number with n under 30 is
     printed in a dimmer style on the panel and prefixed "early:" in
     text. Odin says "thirteen trades so far" out loud.

6.3  What "beating the scorecard" means, per strategy, computed on the
     out-of-sample window and on live paper, not on the fit window:
     * expectancy > 0 after slippage and fees
     * profit factor ≥ 1.3
     * max drawdown ≤ 8% of the trader's $10,000
     * Sharpe ≥ 1.0 over the window
     * at least 30 closed trades in the window
     * the WORST walk-forward fold is still positive expectancy
     * win rate: reported, compared to the strategy's own EXPECTED band
       (section 7) — a strategy whose win rate is far above its band is
       flagged "suspicious", not celebrated, and the Forge checks for
       look-ahead or a broken stop before anyone smiles.

6.4  Graduation gates (documentation only; nothing enables real money):
     G0 CANDIDATE   written down with exact rules
     G1 BACKTESTED  passes 6.3 on 5+ years in-sample
     G2 WALKED      passes 6.3 on every out-of-sample fold (12.3)
     G3 STRESSED    survives Monte Carlo (12.4) and parameter jitter (12.5)
     G4 SHADOW      ran 10 trading days on paper with no order size
                    (signals only, logged), matching the backtest's
                    signal timing within one bar
     G5 PAPER       trading paper money, ≥ 30 closed trades, passes 6.3
     G6 SEASONED    ≥ 90 trading days on paper, ≤ 8% drawdown, passes 6.3
     Odin's report shows each strategy's gate. The readiness tool lists
     G6 as one of its (unmet, documentation-only) conditions.

6.5  The council-level scorecard adds: correlation between traders'
     daily P&L (a council of five that all lose on the same day is one
     trader wearing five hats), portfolio heat (10.3), and "cash drag"
     (how much of the $50,000 total sat idle).


=====================================================================
7.  THE STRATEGY CATALOGUE — FORTY, WITH RULES, BANDS AND FAILURE MODES
=====================================================================

7.0  The universe. Liquid only; nothing that cannot take a $1,000 paper
     order without moving: SPY QQQ IWM DIA XLF XLK XLE XLV XLI XLU XLP
     XLY GLD SLV USO UNG TLT IEF HYG LQD EEM EFA VXX(avoid, decays; use
     only as a regime input) plus the twenty largest S&P 500 names by
     dollar volume, refreshed monthly by the Forge; crypto: BTC/USD,
     ETH/USD, SOL/USD on Alpaca. Options underlyings: SPY, QQQ, IWM only.

7.1  How each strategy is written. One file per strategy in the Forge
     (forge/strategies/<id>.py) and a mirror in the Hall
     (src/strategies/<id>.js) generated by the Forge from the same spec
     so the rules cannot drift. Each spec has: id, family, universe,
     timeframe, entry, exit, stop, sizing rule, parameters with allowed
     ranges, EXPECTED win-rate band, expected trades/year, "dies when"
     (the regime that kills it), and the regime gate it needs (section
     9). The Hall executes; it never reinterprets.

Below, "P" = parameters with their search range for the Forge. Expected
win-rate bands are what honest versions of these strategies show over
long histories; they are there so a result far outside the band is
treated as a bug, not a breakthrough.

FAMILY A — TREND (win rate 35–50%, payoff ratio 2–4, dies in chop)
  A1 MA cross             fast/slow SMA cross, long only. P: fast 10–30,
                          slow 40–120. Exit on cross back. Confirm slow
                          MA slope > 0. Band 38–48%.
  A2 Donchian breakout    long on 20–55-day high, exit on 10–20-day low.
                          Band 35–45%. The classic turtle shape.
  A3 ADX-filtered trend   A1 only when ADX(14) > 20–25. Fewer trades,
                          higher band 42–52%.
  A4 Dual momentum ETF    monthly: hold the best of {SPY, EFA, EEM} by
                          12-month return if above T-bill return, else
                          TLT. Band 55–65% monthly, few trades.
  A5 52-week-high pullback long when within 5% of 52w high AND RSI(2)
                          < 10, exit RSI(2) > 70. Band 60–70%.

FAMILY B — MEAN REVERSION (win rate 60–75%, payoff ratio 0.7–1.2,
                           dies in crashes and strong trends)
  B1 RSI(2) on ETFs        long when RSI(2) < 5–15 and price > SMA(200),
                          exit when close > SMA(5) or RSI(2) > 70–85.
                          Band 62–72%. Stop 3× ATR.
  B2 Bollinger fade       long at −2σ below 20-MA, exit at the MA. Only
                          when price > SMA(200) and VIX < 30. Band 60–70%.
  B3 Gap-down fade        SPY/QQQ open gaps down 0.7–1.5% with VIX < 25,
                          long at open, exit at close or prior close.
                          Band 58–66%.
  B4 3-down-days          three consecutive lower closes above SMA(200),
                          long, exit first up close. Band 65–75%.
  B5 Internal-bar-strength IBS = (close−low)/(high−low) < 0.2 on ETFs
                          above SMA(200), exit IBS > 0.8. Band 60–68%.
  B6 Pairs (XLK/QQQ, GLD/SLV, SPY/IWM) z-score of the log ratio over 20
                          days > 2 → short rich / long cheap (paper
                          allows shorts in ETFs; sim allows too). Exit
                          at z 0. Band 62–72%. Dies when correlation
                          breaks; kill at z > 4.

FAMILY C — MOMENTUM / BREAKOUT (win rate 40–55%, dies in chop)
  C1 Opening range        first 30 min range on SPY/QQQ; long break of
                          the high with volume > 1.5× average, stop at
                          range low, exit at close. Band 42–52%.
  C2 Volatility contraction  NR7 (narrowest range in 7) then break.
                          Band 45–55%.
  C3 Crypto 1h breakout   the existing BTC rule, re-tuned. Band 40–50%.
  C4 Relative strength rotation  weekly: top 3 of the 11 sector ETFs by
                          3-month return, equal weight, rebalance
                          monthly. Band ~55% per month.
  C5 Earnings drift       (large caps) long the day after an earnings
                          gap-up > 5% that closes in the top 20% of its
                          range, hold 10 days. Band 52–60%.

FAMILY D — VOLATILITY (regime plays)
  D1 VIX spike fade       when VIX closes > 30 and then closes lower two
                          days running, long SPY, exit when VIX < 20 or
                          30 days. Band 70–80% but rare (a few a year).
  D2 Term-structure       when VIX < VIX3M (contango) hold SPY; when
                          inverted, flat. Band n/a (a filter, mostly).
  D3 ATR expansion        enter A2 only when ATR(14) rising 5 days.

FAMILY E — SEASONAL / CALENDAR (small edges, use as tilts)
  E1 Turn-of-month        long SPY last 2 sessions of the month through
                          first 3. Band 62–70%.
  E2 FOMC drift           long SPY from the close before an FOMC
                          announcement to the announcement close.
                          Band 60–70%. Dates from FRED/FMP calendar.
  E3 Options-expiry week  fade the Friday? No — research only in the
                          Forge; not promoted this sprint.

FAMILY F — CRYPTO-SPECIFIC
  F1 BTC weekend mean reversion  Saturday/Sunday −3% move, fade to the
                          Friday close. Band 58–66%.
  F2 ETH/BTC ratio momentum  20-day ratio momentum, long the stronger.
  F3 Funding-rate proxy   NOT available on Alpaca; skip. Note in README.

FAMILY G — DEFINED-RISK SHORT PREMIUM (the high-win-rate lane; full
           rules in section 8; win rate 70–85%, payoff ratio 0.3–0.5,
           dies in gap crashes — which is why the loss is capped)
  G1 SPY put credit spread   G2 SPY iron condor   G3 QQQ put credit spread
  G4 IWM iron condor         G5 "the wheel" on SPY (cash-secured put →
                             covered call), paper only.

FAMILY H — PORTFOLIO / ALLOCATION (the council's baseline)
  H1 60/40 SPY/TLT monthly rebalance      the benchmark everything must
                                          beat on a risk-adjusted basis
  H2 Risk parity across SPY/TLT/GLD       inverse-vol weights, monthly
  H3 Buy-and-hold SPY                     the other benchmark

7.2  Forty is the catalogue. The Forge tests all of them. The Hall runs
     the ones the Forge promotes, at most ONE per councillor plus the
     council's benchmark. A trader is a lane, not a pile.

7.3  Every strategy, regardless: a hard stop (or a defined-risk structure
     in G), one position per trader at a time, a cooldown of ≥ 2 bars
     after an exit, position sizing by the risk engine (section 10), no
     averaging down, no pyramiding, no trading in the first 2 minutes or
     the last 2 minutes of the session, no new positions on a day the
     regime engine says "no".


=====================================================================
8.  THE HIGH-WIN-RATE LANE — DEFINED-RISK SHORT PREMIUM, DONE HONESTLY
=====================================================================

This is the only place an "80%" is a real number. Selling a put credit
spread 15–20 delta on SPY, 30–45 days out, wins about 75–85% of the time
over long histories because the market only has to NOT fall a lot. The
catch is the shape: many small wins, occasional larger losses. Two things
make it honest instead of a trap:
  (1) the loss is CAPPED by the long leg — the worst case is known before
      entry and it is what the risk engine sizes against;
  (2) the report shows expectancy and profit factor next to the win rate
      every time, so a run of wins never hides the tail.

8.1  Instruments: SPY, QQQ, IWM options, Alpaca paper, multi-leg orders.
     Nothing else. No single-leg short options, ever (undefined risk).
     No naked calls, ever. No 0DTE, ever.

8.2  G1 — put credit spread
     * Entry: regime engine says "normal" or "calm" (section 9); IV rank
       of the underlying ≥ 20 (from a 252-day window of ATM IV the Forge
       maintains); no earnings/FOMC inside the next 7 days (E-calendar).
     * Structure: sell the put nearest 15–20 delta, buy the put $3–$5
       lower (P: width 3–5), 30–45 DTE (P).
     * Credit: at least 25% of the width (a $5 wide spread must bring in
       ≥ $1.25) or no trade.
     * Size: max loss = width − credit, per contract ×100. The risk engine
       sizes so that max loss ≤ the per-trade risk budget (10.1), and
       total open defined-risk ≤ 30% of that trader's equity.
     * Management: take profit at 50% of credit; stop at 2× credit lost
       (buy back when the spread trades at 2× what we sold it for);
       time exit at 21 DTE regardless.
     * Expected: win rate 72–82%, payoff ratio ≈ 0.35–0.5, profit factor
       1.2–1.6 after fees. If the Forge shows > 88% win rate, it is
       broken; find the bug.
  G2 — iron condor: G1 plus the mirror call spread at 15–20 delta, same
     DTE, only when regime = "calm" and the 20-day realized vol is below
     the 252-day median. Manage each side separately at 50% / 2×.
  G3/G4 — same as G1/G2 on QQQ and IWM. One underlying per trader.
  G5 — the wheel, paper only, on SPY in whole 100-share lots the sim
     permits with $10,000 — it does not (SPY ≈ $600). SKIP G5 on SPY;
     run it on a lower-priced liquid ETF only if the Forge finds one that
     passes; otherwise mark "not feasible at this account size" and move
     on. Do not force it.

8.3  Options data: Alpaca provides options chains and quotes for paper.
     Cache the chain once per tick per underlying, never per contract.
     Compute delta yourself (Black-Scholes with the chain's IV) if the
     feed does not supply it; document which.

8.4  Fees: $0.65 per contract per leg each way in the sim; whatever
     Alpaca paper reports, in the Hall. Report fees separately.

8.5  Assignment risk: in paper, early assignment is simulated rarely;
     handle the case (a short put assigned → you now own 100 shares →
     close them next tick and log ASSIGNED). Never let an assignment
     leave a position the Ledger does not know about (5.4 catches it).

8.6  The whole lane runs on ONE councillor (FRIGGA, id "freya", section
     13) so its win rate is never blended into the trend traders' and
     the council number stays honest.


=====================================================================
9.  THE REGIME ENGINE — THE GATE IN FRONT OF EVERY STRATEGY
=====================================================================

Computed once after each close (and once at the open from overnight
data), stored as ONE KV key regime:today, read by every tick:

  9.1 Volatility bucket   VIX close: calm < 15, normal 15–22, elevated
                          22–30, storm > 30. Also realized 20-day vol
                          of SPY and its percentile over 252 days.
  9.2 Trend state         SPY close vs SMA(50) and SMA(200), and the
                          slope of each over 10 days: uptrend / downtrend
                          / range. QQQ the same. Crypto: BTC vs its own
                          MAs.
  9.3 Breadth             % of the 11 sector ETFs above their SMA(50);
                          % of the top-20 names above SMA(20).
  9.4 Term structure      VIX vs VIX3M if obtainable from FRED/FMP;
                          else skip and say so.
  9.5 Event calendar      FOMC dates, CPI/NFP release days, quad-witching,
                          holidays, earnings for any single name in the
                          universe (FMP). Written once a month.
  9.6 The gate table (config, editable):
        family   calm    normal  elevated  storm
        A trend   on      on      on        off
        B meanrev on      on      off       off      (B3 gap fade: on
                                                      only ≤ normal)
        C momo    on      on      on        off
        D vol     off     off     on        on       (D1 lives here)
        E season  on      on      on        off
        F crypto  on      on      on        reduce size 50%
        G premium on      on      off       off      (G2 condor: calm
                                                      only)
        H alloc   always
      Plus: no new positions on FOMC/CPI/NFP days before the release; no
      G-lane entries inside 7 days of an event on the underlying.
  9.7 Odin says the regime in one line at the open: "Normal vol, uptrend,
      breadth strong, FOMC Wednesday — premium lane paused until
      Thursday." He never says why the market will do something. He says
      what the gate is.


=====================================================================
10. THE RISK ENGINE — THE PART THAT LETS THE ANIMAL BE AGGRESSIVE
=====================================================================

10.1 Per trade: risk 1% of the trader's equity ($100 on $10,000) from
     entry to stop; for defined-risk spreads, max loss = the risk. Size
     = risk ÷ (entry − stop) rounded DOWN to whole shares/contracts;
     if that is zero, no trade.
10.2 Volatility scaling: for A/C families, stop = 2× ATR(14) (P: 1.5–3);
     for B, 3× ATR or the structural level, whichever is closer.
10.3 Portfolio heat: sum of open risk across the council ≤ 4% of total
     equity ($2,000 of $50,000). New entries blocked above that.
10.4 Day-trade guard: equities/ETFs — a position opened today is not
     closed today except by its stop; the PDT counter is tracked and a
     4th day trade in 5 days is refused with a log line. Crypto and
     options have their own rules (options: never open and close the
     same spread the same day except at the stop).
10.5 Daily loss limit per trader: −2% of equity closes all of that
     trader's positions and halts it until the next session. Council
     daily loss limit −3% halts the council. Weekly −5% halts until
     Monday and Odin says so in Telegram.
10.6 Drawdown governor: at 5% drawdown from peak, every size halves. At
     8%, the trader stops opening positions until it has been reviewed
     by the Forge and re-promoted (the report shows "in review").
10.7 Correlation guard: no two traders long the same direction in SPY
     and QQQ at the same time unless the combined risk fits 10.3 and the
     regime is "uptrend + calm/normal". GLD/SLV, SPY/IWM same rule.
10.8 Streak guard: after 5 consecutive losses in one strategy, its size
     halves for the next 5 trades; after 8, it goes to shadow (signals
     only) and the Forge re-runs it.
10.9 Fat-finger guard: no order larger than 25% of the trader's equity
     in notional; no options order with more than 5 contracts per leg;
     no order at a price more than 2% away from the last quote.
10.10 Every refusal is logged with the rule number. Odin can be asked
     "why didn't Fandral trade today" and answer from the log.


=====================================================================
11. EXECUTION — WHAT HAPPENS ON A TICK, EXACTLY
=====================================================================

Every 5 minutes during the session (crypto: every 15 minutes, all week):
  1. If the market is closed for that trader's instrument → return. One
     read, zero writes.
  2. Read regime:today (one read). If "storm" and the family is off →
     manage existing positions only.
  3. Read the trader's portfolio (one read). Fetch quotes for its open
     symbol(s) in one batched call. Update unrealized in memory.
  4. Check stops / targets / time exits / assignment → if any fires,
     place the closing order, wait for the fill status (one read),
     write the portfolio ONCE, append one trade line.
  5. If flat and the strategy's signal fires on the completed bar (never
     the forming bar) and every risk rule passes → place the order with
     a clientOrderId, wait for status, write ONCE. If partial → keep the
     remainder open for at most 2 ticks, then cancel the remainder and
     size the stop to the filled quantity.
  6. If nothing changed → write nothing. Not the equity, not a timestamp.
  7. At the close: one equity sample per trader, one journal line per
     trader (13.4), one report marker.
  8. Any exception → catch, log with the trader id and rule, never let
     one trader's error stop another's tick, never write a half-state.


=====================================================================
12. THE FORGE — WHERE ODIN GETS SMART
=====================================================================

C:\Asgard\forge\ — Python 3.12 venv, wheels only. Scheduled task at 02:00
Pacific nightly and `forge run` on demand. Logs to C:\Asgard\logs\forge\.

12.1 Data layer: pull and cache to Parquet (section 4.8). One loader
     function per source. Every bar row carries its source and fetch
     time. A "gaps" report lists any missing sessions per symbol.

12.2 Backtest engine: use backtesting.py for single-asset rule strategies
     (simple, actively maintained) and PyBroker for anything that needs
     walk-forward or ML features (walk-forward is pre-wired there).
     vectorbt's open-source version is in maintenance mode (development
     moved to the paid PRO tier) — use it only for fast parameter sweeps
     if PyBroker is too slow, and pin the version. Do not use backtrader
     (frozen). NautilusTrader is more than we need; note it under Later.
     The same fill/slippage/fee model as section 5.5 is used here —
     implement it ONCE in forge/fills.py and have every engine call it.

12.3 Walk-forward: for each strategy, anchored expanding-window folds —
     fit on 3 years, test on the next 6 months, roll forward 6 months,
     across the whole history. Parameters are chosen on the fit window
     ONLY by expectancy-per-trade with a drawdown penalty, never by win
     rate. Report each fold's out-of-sample scorecard. The strategy's
     "walked" verdict is the WORST fold, not the average.

12.4 Monte Carlo: shuffle the out-of-sample trade sequence 2,000 times
     and skip 10% of trades at random each run; report the 5th-percentile
     drawdown and the probability of a 10% drawdown. A strategy whose
     5th-percentile drawdown exceeds 12% is not promoted.

12.5 Parameter stability: for the chosen parameters, jitter each ±20%
     and re-run; if the expectancy sign flips or profit factor falls
     below 1.1 in more than a third of the jittered runs, the parameters
     are a lucky point on a cliff — do not promote; widen the search.

12.6 Look-ahead and leakage checks, automated: every indicator is
     computed with data up to the PREVIOUS completed bar; a unit test
     shifts every input by one bar and asserts the signals shift with
     it. Any strategy whose backtest win rate exceeds its expected band
     by more than 15 points is quarantined until the test passes.

12.7 The tournament and promotion file. Every night:
     * re-run every strategy in the catalogue on data through yesterday
     * produce forge/out/verdicts.json: per strategy — gate reached (G0–
       G3 from the Forge; G4–G6 come from the Hall's live record), the
       scorecard on the last out-of-sample fold, the chosen parameters,
       the regime families it is allowed in, and a one-line plain-English
       verdict ("passes; weakest fold PF 1.22; promote to FANDRAL lane")
     * sign the file (HMAC with a secret only the Forge and the Hall
       hold; wrangler secret put FORGE_HMAC < file) and upload it to the
       Hall's POST /forge/verdicts. The Hall verifies the signature and
       stores it as ONE KV key.
     * The Hall assigns strategies to lanes ONLY from that file, and only
       at the next session open, and only if the strategy's gate is ≥ G3.
       A strategy currently trading that loses its G3 in the nightly run
       is moved to shadow at the next open, positions managed to exit by
       their own rules. Odin reports every promotion and demotion.

12.8 Research that is NOT promoted this sprint but is built and run so
     Odin has real answers: E3, F2, C5, the SEC insider-buying overlay,
     a simple ML filter (gradient-boosted "will this B1 signal win?"
     classifier on 20 features, walk-forward, PyBroker) — reported as
     research, gate G1 at most, never traded until a later brief.

12.9 The Forge writes docs/RESEARCH.md nightly: a human-readable table of
     all forty, their gate, their weakest-fold numbers, and the sentence.
     That file is the thing I can read on my phone.


=====================================================================
13. THE COUNCIL, UPGRADED — FIVE LANES, ONE JUDGE
=====================================================================

The five keep their names and their ids. Each gets a LANE (a family and
a universe), a scorecard, and a nightly self-review. Odin is the judge.

  VOLSTAGG   id "baldr"    Lane: TREND (family A) on SPY/QQQ/sector ETFs.
                           The heavy one. Few trades, big winners.
  HEIMDALL   id "vidar"    Lane: MEAN REVERSION (family B) on ETFs.
                           Sees the dip; many small wins.
  FANDRAL    id "tyr"      Lane: MOMENTUM/BREAKOUT (C) and crypto (F).
                           Quick, aggressive, tightly stopped.
  HOGUN      id "heimdall" Lane: VOLATILITY + SEASONAL (D, E). Quiet most
                           days; acts on regime and calendar.
  FRIGGA     id "freya"    Lane: DEFINED-RISK PREMIUM (G). The high-win-
                           rate lane, on its own so it cannot flatter
                           the others.
  (The benchmark lane H runs as "the hall" itself, not as a councillor.)

13.1 Each lane runs at most one promoted strategy at a time, chosen by
     the Forge's verdicts. The lane's history is continuous even when
     the strategy inside it changes (the Ledger records which strategy
     placed each trade).

13.2 The judge: Odin's nightly review (one Claude call, cheap tier, after
     the close, over the day's trade lines and the regime) writes ONE
     journal line per trader: what it did, whether it followed its rules
     (the log proves it), one observation. Not advice. Not prediction.
     "Heimdall took two B1 entries, both stopped, both in an 'elevated'
     regime the gate should have blocked — gate check failed on the
     second; fixed at 15:41." That is what a journal line looks like.

13.3 The council meeting (Sunday, one Claude call, standard tier): reads
     the week's journal lines, the scorecards and the Forge's verdicts,
     and produces the Sunday report (section 15.3). It is allowed to
     PROPOSE a gate-table change or a lane reassignment, written as a
     proposal in KV; nothing changes until I say "Odin, approve
     proposal 3" — and even then only config, never code, never money.

13.4 Self-review taxonomy (so post-mortems are comparable): every closed
     loss is tagged by the Hall automatically with one of: STOP (hit the
     stop as designed), GAP (gapped through the stop), REGIME (entered in
     a regime the gate should have blocked), TIME (time exit at a loss),
     SLIP (loss caused by fill vs signal difference > 0.3%), RULE (a risk
     rule was violated — should never happen; if it does, halt), OTHER.
     Odin reports the tag counts. A month of GAP losses means the family
     is wrong for the regime; a month of SLIP means the fill model lies.

13.5 Making them "smarter" without lying about it: the councillors do
     not learn by feeling. They get smarter because (a) the Forge
     replaces their parameters with walk-forward-stable ones, (b) the
     regime gate stops them trading when their family loses, (c) the
     risk engine stops one bad week from becoming a bad month, (d) the
     journal makes every loss explainable, and (e) the tournament
     retires a strategy the moment a better one passes the same gates.
     That is intelligence you can audit. Say it that way in the README.


=====================================================================
14. THE ANTI-OVERFIT CHECKLIST — RUN BEFORE ANYTHING IS PROMOTED
=====================================================================

  [ ] parameters chosen on fit windows only; out-of-sample never touched
  [ ] worst fold, not average, is the verdict
  [ ] fewer than 4 free parameters per strategy
  [ ] each parameter's range was set BEFORE the search and is in the spec
  [ ] ≥ 100 trades in the full-history backtest, ≥ 30 per fold
  [ ] fills at next open, slippage and fees applied, partials simulated
  [ ] one-bar shift test passes (12.6)
  [ ] Monte Carlo 5th-percentile drawdown ≤ 12%
  [ ] ±20% parameter jitter keeps expectancy positive in ≥ 2/3 of runs
  [ ] win rate inside the family's expected band (or explained)
  [ ] survivorship: ETF universe only, plus current large caps with a
      note that single names carry survivorship bias — no promotion of
      a single-name strategy this sprint
  [ ] the same strategy on a "cousin" instrument (SPY↔QQQ, GLD↔SLV)
      shows the same sign of expectancy
  [ ] a human sentence explains WHY the edge should exist (behavioural,
      structural, calendar). "It backtested well" is not a why.


=====================================================================
15. WHAT ODIN SAYS — REPORTS THAT CANNOT LIE
=====================================================================

15.1 Morning (06:25 Pacific, Telegram + spoken on request): the regime
     line (9.7); each lane's status (strategy, gate, position or flat,
     yesterday's P&L); the council's week-to-date; any halts; the call
     budget; "PAPER · SIMULATED" in the first line.
15.2 Close (13:15 Pacific): trades today with tags; each lane's scorecard
     for the last 30 trades with n; council equity, realized and
     unrealized separately; drawdown from peak; refusals count with the
     top rule; KV writes today; one journal line per trader (13.2).
15.3 Sunday (10:00 Pacific): the week; the tournament's promotions and
     demotions with one sentence each; the anti-overfit checklist result
     for anything promoted; the money board (16.8); proposals awaiting
     "approve"; the holiday-list warning if due.
15.4 On demand, by voice or text: "Odin, how's the hall" → the close
     report's first three lines. "Odin, why didn't Volstagg trade" → the
     refusal log. "Odin, what's Frigga's win rate" → "Seventy-eight
     percent on forty-one trades, expectancy plus nineteen dollars,
     profit factor one point four. Paper, simulated." Always the
     denominator, always expectancy beside win rate, always "paper".
15.5 Never: a prediction, a recommendation for my own account, a number
     without a source in the Ledger, a win rate without n, a blended
     realized/unrealized figure.
15.6 The panel on Odin's page: the five lanes as five cards (name, id in
     small type, strategy, gate badge G0–G6, position, 30-trade scorecard
     with the seven numbers), the council equity curve, the regime strip
     across the top, "PAPER · SIMULATED" as a permanent banner. No
     restructuring of the page; extend what polls /paper-trading/status.

  * Every idea is rated on: FIRST DOLLAR (how many days to the first real
    payment, honestly), CAPITAL (cash needed before that), HOURS/DAY of
    MY time, ASGARD SHARE (how much Odin/Thor/Loki can actually do), and
    TRAP (the thing that gets people banned, sued or broke).

    that require cold-texting or AI cold-calling, or that repost content
    without a licence are listed ONLY so I know why they are out.

    numbers, drafts the words, and reports. I do the selling and I press
    every button that moves money or publishes. Anything that would post
    or send ships disabled behind "Odin, approve".
  * "Advertised" prices from agencies are what agencies ASK, not what a
    first-time seller closes. Where a number is advertised I say so.

16.2 THE TOP SIX, BUILT OUT (Odin builds the tooling for all six in the
     21 days; I run whichever I choose; the money board tracks them)

  DEEP DIVE 1 — FLIP TO FIRST DOLLAR (idea 1)
    Why first: it is the only one on the list that reliably pays inside
    a week with $50, and it teaches the money board with real dollars.
    Odin builds: a "flip desk" tool set —
      * flip_scan: I paste an item title/photo description; Odin returns
        eBay sold comps (last 30 days, via search — no scraping behind
        logins), a suggested max buy, expected net after eBay's fee
        (~13.6% + $0.30) and shipping, and a GO/NO.
      * flip_list: drafts the listing title, bullets, and a fair price;
        I post it (Odin never posts).
      * flip_ledger: every item — bought, listed, sold, net — feeds the
        money board.
    Day plan: D1 scan 20 local listings, buy 3 (≤ $50 total) that show
    ≥ 40% net; D2 list all 3; D3–7 sell, ship; D7 the first real dollar
    is on the board. Traps: counterfeits (no branded electronics/shoes
    at first), meet in public, cash only.

  DEEP DIVE 2 — WHOP, DONE RIGHT (idea 2)
    What is already true (from my docs): rates $0.20–$6 per 1,000 views
    advertised; the realised blended rate across a large dataset was
    about $0.39; the average clipper's LIFETIME earnings were about $305;
    Whop takes 7%; there is a 24-hour hold plus anti-bot review; fixed
    pools run out; hidden likes = automatic rejection; clips are reviewed
    AFTER views accrue, so rejections happen with views already spent.
    So the honest shape is small: 2–4 accounts, one campaign at a time,
    every brief rule enforced in code, and the goal is $50–300/month
    while the pipeline is proven — not a media company.
    Odin builds/updates: the existing clips_*/vizard_* pipeline behind a
    campaign_brief object that refuses to publish anything that violates
    the brief (hashtag count, disclosure line placement, approved line
    present, length ≥ 10 s, platform allowed); a "campaign board" that
    lists open Whop campaigns daily with rate, pool remaining, category,
    and a NO for gambling/casino/anything TikTok prohibits; per-clip
    status (posted → verified → paid) and the realised $/1K on the board.
    Publishing stays behind "Odin, approve clip 12".
    Day plan: D2 board live; D3 accounts checked (likes visible); D4
    first 5 clips approved by me and posted; D5–14 daily; D14 first
    payout state known (paid / pending / rejected, and why).

  DEEP DIVE 3 — MISSED-CALL TEXT-BACK + GBP (idea 3)
    The pitch to a plumber: "you miss calls; each one is a $300 job; I
    text them back in 10 seconds and book them." Tools cost $20–150/mo
    (Twilio-based build is cheaper: I already have Twilio). Charge
    $97–197/mo. One lost job a month pays it.
    Compliance is the whole game: the text goes ONLY to a number that
    just called the business (inbound-initiated, transactional), signed
    with the business name, with STOP handling, on a registered A2P
    10DLC campaign for the BUSINESS's brand. No cold texts to anyone,
    ever. No AI voice calling out. GBP work is manual clean-up: hours,
    categories, photos, services, a compliant review link.
    Odin builds: mctb — the Twilio flow (missed call → SMS within 10 s →
    two-way relay to the owner's phone → daily summary), a 10DLC
    registration checklist, an onboarding form, a monthly "calls
    missed / texted / replied / booked" report per client, a Stripe
    payment link generator (I create Stripe; Odin never charges anyone).
    Day plan: D3 the flow works on my own number; D4 the one-page offer
    and a 20-business list from Google Maps (public business numbers,
    for me to CALL by voice or visit — not text); D5–10 I pitch 5/day in
    person or by phone; D10 first client at $97; D14 their first report.

  DEEP DIVE 4 — AI VIDEO/EDIT SERVICES (idea 4)
    The buyer: local businesses and small creators who have long video
    and no time. The offer: 10 captioned shorts from one long video,
    48-hour turnaround, $150; or $400/month for 30. Fiverr/Upwork for
    reach; local for margin.
    Odin builds: the cut pipeline (Vizard/ffmpeg from the clipping work,
    repointed at CLIENT-OWNED footage with a licence line in the order
    form), caption styles, ElevenLabs voice-over from my designed voices,
    a delivery page per order, a proof-of-work log. I review every cut
    before delivery.
    Day plan: D2 portfolio of 3 samples from my own footage; D3 Fiverr +
    Upwork listings and a local one-pager; D4–10 pitch 3/day; D10 first
    paid order.

  DEEP DIVE 5 — CREATOR BOUNTIES (idea 5)
    I joined a Krea campaign already. Odin keeps a bounty board:
    Krea, brand UGC boards on Whop, platform creator programs — with
    deadline, format, payout, rights granted, and a fit score against
    what I can make in a day. Odin drafts the concept, the script, the
    caption, and the disclosure. I make and submit. Track submitted →
    accepted → paid.
    Day plan: D1 board live; D2 the Krea sample scripted; D3 made and
    submitted; ongoing 1/day when fit ≥ 7/10.

  DEEP DIVE 6 — PHOTOGRAPHER WHITE-LABEL (idea 6)
    My own research says: sell to the 15–30 Kern County photographers,
    not the 2,000 agents; they own the photos, the relationship and the
    invoice; the AI walkthrough is a $50–150 upsell they add. Legal:
    no cold texts (TCPA; texts are calls in the Ninth Circuit), no AI
    voice cold calls (CA), compliant email + manual calls only; CA AB
    723 disclosure for AI-altered listing IMAGES and DRE's advisory that
    §10176 applies to video anyway — so every deliverable ships with the
    disclosure line and a link to the originals, which is itself the
    wedge ("we're the ones who do it by the book").
    Odin builds: the sample walkthrough from public-domain interior
    photos, the one-page offer with the disclosure built in, the
    photographer list from public directories (for me to email/call),
    the order form with the rights line, the delivery page.
    Day plan: D5 samples; D6 list of 25; D7–14 I email 5/day (CAN-SPAM
    compliant, opt-out) and call 2/day live; D14 first pilot.

16.3 THE MONEY BOARD (built in the Hall, shown on Odin's page and in the
     Sunday report). One row per venture: status (idea / building /
     live / paused / killed), first-dollar date (real, from a payment),
     revenue to date, cost to date, hours logged (I say "Odin, log two
     hours on flips"), profit, $/hour, next action. Rules: a venture with
     no first dollar 14 days after "live" is marked "review" and Odin
     says so; 21 days → "kill unless approved". Odin never invents a
     number here either — revenue is entered from a real payment I
     confirm, or it is zero.

16.4 THE OUTREACH LAW, in code and in the README: no SMS to anyone who
     did not text/call first; no autodialed or AI-voice outbound calls;
     email only with a real opt-out and a real address; no scraping
     behind logins; no fake reviews, no incentivised reviews without
     disclosure; no reposting content without a licence; no gambling
     promotion anywhere. Any tool that could break these refuses with the
     rule quoted.

16.5 What Odin actually does each morning for the money side: refresh
     the bounty board and the Whop board; list any flips that sold or
     messages that need my reply (drafted, not sent); the money board's
     three biggest movers; ONE suggested next action per live venture.
     Ninety seconds of reading. Then he goes back to the hall.

16.6 Later (in the README, unbuilt): ideas 10, 11, 24, 33, 34, 40 — the
     compounding ones — once any of the six above has paid twice.

16.7 What is NOT here on purpose: real-money trading (see rule 0), prop-
     firm evaluations (a later conversation after gate G6, never inside
     this brief), leverage, credit, borrowing, anything with a monthly
     subscription cost before the first dollar except the $5 Cloudflare
     plan I already pay.


=====================================================================
17. THE TWENTY-ONE DAYS
=====================================================================

Day 1   Read the repo, every doc in docs/, and this brief. Write
        docs/PLAN.md restating this brief in your own words with any
        decision you had to make. Set up C:\Asgard\forge\ (venv, wheels).
        Create the Alpaca paper account steps in NEXT-STEPS.txt. Start
        the history pull (4.8). Money: flip desk tools (16.2 DD1).
Day 2   Fill/slippage/fee model in forge/fills.py and mirrored in the

18. HOW WE KNOW IT IS DONE
=====================================================================

Automated (a script prints PASS/FAIL for each):
  [ ] LiveBroker throws on every method; grep proves no caller exists;
      no file contains a real brokerage key; readiness reports
      "no live path"
  [ ] every strategy spec has an expected band, a "dies when", a gate
      family, ≤ 4 parameters with pre-set ranges
  [ ] one-bar shift test passes for all forty
  [ ] fills.py is the only fill model and both engines import it
  [ ] walk-forward verdict = worst fold, and the report prints it
  [ ] a fabricated strategy with look-ahead (a test fixture that peeks
      at the next bar) is QUARANTINED by 12.6, not promoted
  [ ] risk rules 10.1–10.9 each have a unit test that triggers them
  [ ] the tick writes zero KV keys when nothing changed (mock test)
  [ ] a busy simulated day writes ≤ 60 keys
  [ ] every printed win rate carries "(n=…)"; a report with a win rate
      and no expectancy beside it fails the report test
  [ ] "PAPER · SIMULATED" appears in the first line of every report and
      permanently on the panel
  [ ] verdicts.json with a bad signature is rejected; with a good one,
      lanes update at the next open and not before
  [ ] reconcile mismatch halts the trader and appears in the report
  [ ] call budget: the 91st call of a 90-cap source is refused and logged
  [ ] money tools: any attempt to send SMS to a non-inbound number,
      post without approval, or exceed the brief's hashtag count is
      refused with the rule quoted
  [ ] the money board shows zero revenue until a payment is confirmed

Manual (in NEXT-STEPS.txt):
  [ ] "Odin, how's the hall" → regime line + five lanes + PAPER
  [ ] "Odin, what's Frigga's win rate" → number with n and expectancy
  [ ] "Odin, why didn't Volstagg trade" → the refusal log line
  [ ] "Odin, approve proposal 1" → config changes, nothing else
  [ ] the Sunday report arrives on Telegram at 10:00 Pacific
  [ ] the panel shows five cards, gate badges, the equity curve, the
      banner
  [ ] flip_scan on a real listing returns comps and a GO/NO
  [ ] the Whop board lists real open campaigns with pools and rates
  [ ] mctb texts my phone within 10 s of a missed call to the test number
  [ ] the bounty board shows the Krea campaign with its deadline

    that could switch to live. If a test needs it, the test is wrong.

    print a win rate without n and expectancy.

    without "Odin, approve". Do not cold-text. Do not AI-cold-call.

    Loki's tools.

  * Do not give me partial files. Do not say "done" before section 18.

20. WORKED SPECS — THREE STRATEGIES WRITTEN OUT EXACTLY
=====================================================================

The generator (7.1) reads specs like these from forge/strategies/*.json
and writes both the Python and the JavaScript. Copy this shape for all
forty. Anything not expressible in this shape needs a new field added to
the SCHEMA first (forge/schema.json), never an ad-hoc hack in one file.

20.1  A1 — moving-average cross, trend family

{
  "id": "A1_ma_cross",
  "family": "A",
  "name": "MA cross",
  "why": "Prices trend more often than a random walk would because
          capital arrives slowly; a fast/slow crossover is a blunt but
          honest way to be long only when the slow trend agrees.",
  "universe": ["SPY","QQQ","XLK","XLF","XLE","GLD"],
  "timeframe": "1d",
  "side": "long_only",
  "params": {
    "fast": {"default": 20, "min": 10, "max": 30, "step": 5},
    "slow": {"default": 50, "min": 40, "max": 120, "step": 10},
    "atr_stop": {"default": 2.0, "min": 1.5, "max": 3.0, "step": 0.5}
  },
  "indicators": {
    "fast_ma": "SMA(close, fast)",
    "slow_ma": "SMA(close, slow)",
    "slow_slope": "slow_ma[0] - slow_ma[-10]",
    "atr": "ATR(14)"
  },
  "entry": "cross_above(fast_ma, slow_ma) AND slow_slope > 0",
  "exit":  "cross_below(fast_ma, slow_ma)",
  "stop":  "entry_price - atr_stop * atr",
  "time_exit_bars": null,
  "cooldown_bars": 2,
  "regime_families": ["calm","normal","elevated"],
  "expected": {"win_rate": [0.38, 0.48], "payoff": [2.0, 4.0],
               "trades_per_year": [4, 10]},
  "dies_when": "range-bound markets; whipsaws around the slow MA",
  "lane": "VOLSTAGG"
}

20.2  B1 — RSI(2) pullback, mean-reversion family

{
  "id": "B1_rsi2_pullback",
  "family": "B",
  "name": "RSI(2) pullback above the 200",
  "why": "Short, sharp dips inside a long uptrend are usually bought;
          RSI(2) measures the dip, SMA(200) confirms the uptrend.",
  "universe": ["SPY","QQQ","IWM","DIA","XLK","XLV","XLP"],
  "timeframe": "1d",
  "side": "long_only",
  "params": {
    "rsi_entry": {"default": 10, "min": 5, "max": 15, "step": 5},
    "rsi_exit":  {"default": 70, "min": 65, "max": 85, "step": 5},
    "atr_stop":  {"default": 3.0, "min": 2.0, "max": 4.0, "step": 0.5}
  },
  "indicators": {
    "rsi2": "RSI(close, 2)",
    "sma200": "SMA(close, 200)",
    "sma5": "SMA(close, 5)",
    "atr": "ATR(14)"
  },
  "entry": "rsi2 < rsi_entry AND close > sma200",
  "exit":  "close > sma5 OR rsi2 > rsi_exit",
  "stop":  "entry_price - atr_stop * atr",
  "time_exit_bars": 10,
  "cooldown_bars": 2,
  "regime_families": ["calm","normal"],
  "expected": {"win_rate": [0.62, 0.72], "payoff": [0.7, 1.2],
               "trades_per_year": [15, 40]},
  "dies_when": "crashes (the dip keeps dipping) and vol > 30",
  "lane": "HEIMDALL"
}

20.3  G1 — put credit spread, defined-risk premium family

{
  "id": "G1_put_credit_spread",
  "family": "G",
  "name": "SPY put credit spread 15-20 delta",
  "why": "Option sellers are paid for taking gap risk; with the long
          leg the gap risk is capped, so the premium is a bounded bet
          that the market does not fall a lot in the next month.",
  "universe": ["SPY"],
  "timeframe": "1d",
  "side": "short_premium_defined",
  "structure": {
    "legs": [
      {"action": "sell", "type": "put", "delta_target": [0.15, 0.20]},
      {"action": "buy",  "type": "put", "width_below": "width"}
    ],
    "dte": {"default": 38, "min": 30, "max": 45}
  },
  "params": {
    "width": {"default": 5, "min": 3, "max": 5, "step": 1},
    "min_credit_frac": {"default": 0.25, "min": 0.20, "max": 0.33,
                        "step": 0.03},
    "take_profit_frac": {"default": 0.50, "min": 0.40, "max": 0.60,
                         "step": 0.05},
    "stop_mult": {"default": 2.0, "min": 1.5, "max": 2.5, "step": 0.25}
  },
  "indicators": {
    "iv_rank": "IVRank(atm_iv, 252)",
    "days_to_event": "DaysToNextEvent(calendar, ['FOMC','CPI','NFP'])"
  },
  "entry": "flat AND iv_rank >= 20 AND days_to_event > 7 AND
            credit >= min_credit_frac * width",
  "exit":  "spread_value <= (1 - take_profit_frac) * credit",
  "stop":  "spread_value >= stop_mult * credit",
  "time_exit_dte": 21,
  "cooldown_bars": 1,
  "regime_families": ["calm","normal"],
  "risk": {"max_loss_per_contract": "(width - credit) * 100",
           "size_rule": "max_loss <= per_trade_risk",
           "max_open_defined_risk_frac": 0.30},
  "expected": {"win_rate": [0.72, 0.82], "payoff": [0.35, 0.50],
               "profit_factor": [1.2, 1.6], "trades_per_year": [10, 14]},
  "dies_when": "gap crashes; sustained vol > 30; earnings/FOMC surprises",
  "lane": "FRIGGA"
}

=====================================================================
21. REPORT TEMPLATES — THE EXACT WORDS, WITH THE BLANKS
=====================================================================

Every blank is filled from the Ledger or left as "—". Nothing else may
appear. If a value is missing, the line says "— (no data)". The report
test (18) fails if a win rate appears without "(n=" on the same line or
without "exp" on the same line.

21.1  MORNING

  PAPER · SIMULATED — Odin, {date} {time} PT
  Regime: {vol_bucket} vol (VIX {vix}), {trend_state}, breadth {breadth}%
          {event_line}
  Lanes:
    VOLSTAGG  {strategy_id or "—"}  {gate}  {position or "flat"}
              yday {pnl_yday:+$}
    HEIMDALL  …
    FANDRAL   …
    HOGUN     …
    FRIGGA    …
  Council: wtd {pnl_wtd:+$}  equity ${equity} (realized ${realized},
           unrealized ${unrealized})  dd {drawdown}% from peak
  Halts: {halts or "none"}
  Budget: Alpaca {n}/{cap}, TwelveData {n}/{cap}, KV writes yday {n}
  Money: {money_one_line}

21.2  CLOSE

  PAPER · SIMULATED — Odin, close {date}
  Trades today: {count}
    {time} {lane} {strategy} {side} {symbol} {qty} @ {fill} → {exit or
    "open"} {pnl:+$} [{tag}]
    …
  Lane scorecards (last 30 closed):
    VOLSTAGG  n={n} win {win_rate}% (n={n}) exp {exp:+$}/{expR:+R}
              pf {pf} dd {dd}% avgW ${avg_win} avgL ${avg_loss}
              {gate} {broker}
    …
  Council: equity ${equity}  realized ${realized}  unrealized
           ${unrealized}  dd {dd}%  heat {heat}%  cash drag {drag}%
  Refusals: {count} (top: rule {rule} ×{k})
  KV writes today: {n}
  Journal:
    VOLSTAGG — {journal_line}
    …
  Regime for tomorrow: {regime_line}

21.3  SUNDAY

  PAPER · SIMULATED — Odin, week of {monday} to {friday}
  Week: council {pnl_week:+$} ({pct:+%}), best lane {lane} {pnl:+$},
        worst lane {lane} {pnl:+$}, max dd this week {dd}%
  Tournament:
    promoted   {strategy} → {lane} ({one_sentence})
    demoted    {strategy} from {lane} ({one_sentence})
    unchanged  {k} strategies
  Anti-overfit check on promotions: {pass_count}/{total} lines PASS
    {any_fail_line}
  Gates: {G6:k} {G5:k} {G4:k} {G3:k} {G2:k} {G1:k} {G0:k}
  Money board:
    {venture}  {status}  1st$ {date or "—"}  rev ${rev}  cost ${cost}
               hrs {hrs}  $/hr {rate}  next: {next_action}
    …
  Proposals awaiting "Odin, approve N": {list or "none"}
  Warnings: {holiday_list_warning or "none"}; {data_gaps or "none"}

21.4  SPOKEN LINES (the voice versions; twelve words or fewer unless
      asked a real question)
  "How's the hall"        → "Normal vol, uptrend. Council up {x} this
                             week. Paper, simulated."
  "{lane}'s win rate"     → "{pct} percent on {n} trades, expectancy
                             {sign} {dollars}. Paper."
  "Why didn't {lane} trade" → "Refused by rule {rule}: {short_reason}."
  "Anything for me"       → "{money_one_line}."
  "Approve proposal {n}"  → "Proposal {n} applied: {one_line}." or
                             "No proposal {n}."

21.5  JOURNAL LINE FORMAT (one per trader per day, ≤ 240 characters):
  "{did}; rules {followed|violated rule N}; {one_observation}."

21.6  REFUSAL LOG LINE FORMAT:
  "{ts} {lane} {strategy} REFUSED rule {n} {name}: {values}"
  e.g. "14:35 HEIMDALL B1 REFUSED rule 10.3 heat: open 3.9% + new 1.0%
        > 4.0%"

22. THE MONEY TOOLS — EXACT NAMES, INPUTS, AND WHAT THEY REFUSE
=====================================================================

All added to ODIN_TOOLS. Every one that could publish, send, or spend
ships DISABLED and returns "needs 'Odin, approve …'" until I say it.

  flip_scan({title, condition, asking_price, notes})
      → comps (last-30-day solds via search), suggested max buy,
        expected net after fees + shipping, GO/NO with one reason.
  flip_list({item_id})           → title, bullets, price. Never posts.
  flip_ledger({op, item_id, ...}) → bought/listed/sold/net; feeds board.

  campaign_board()               → open Whop campaigns: rate, pool
                                    remaining, category, allowed
                                    platforms, brief rules, FIT/NO.
                                    Refuses gambling/casino/adult.
  campaign_brief_set({campaign_id, rules})   → stores the brief.
  clip_prepare({source, campaign_id})        → cut + caption that
                                    PASSES the brief or an error naming
                                    the rule it would break.
  clip_publish({clip_id})        → DISABLED. Needs "Odin, approve clip N".
  clip_status()                  → posted / verified / paid / rejected,
                                    realised $/1K.

  bounty_board()                 → open creator campaigns with deadline,
                                    format, payout, rights, fit score.
  bounty_draft({campaign_id})    → concept, script, caption, disclosure.

  mctb_setup({business_name, forward_to, hours})  → the Twilio flow
                                    config; A2P 10DLC checklist.
  mctb_report({client_id, period}) → missed / texted / replied / booked.
  mctb_send(...)                 → does not exist. Texts go ONLY from the
                                    flow, ONLY to a number that just
                                    called. There is no manual send tool.

  edit_order({source_url_or_file, style, count, licence_ack:true})
      → refuses without licence_ack; produces cuts + captions; delivery
        page link. I review before delivery.
  offer_page({venture})          → the one-page offer text; I publish.

  outreach_list({venture, area}) → public business names/phones/emails
                                    from public directories for ME to
                                    call or email. Never texts. Never
                                    stores anything from behind a login.
  email_draft({venture, to, template}) → CAN-SPAM-compliant draft with
                                    real address + opt-out. DISABLED to
                                    send; I send from my own mail.

  money_board()                  → the board (16.3).
  money_log({venture, field, value, evidence}) → revenue requires
                                    evidence = a payment reference I
                                    type; hours require none.

  Every tool logs {tool, args_hash, refused?, rule} to the tool log.

=====================================================================
25. GLOSSARY — SO I CAN READ WHAT ODIN SAYS (goes in docs/GLOSSARY.md)
=====================================================================

  Paper trading     Fake money, real prices. Every number Odin gives me
                    is from this. No real money exists in this system.
  Win rate          Out of every 100 closed trades, how many made money.
                    On its own it means almost nothing.
  Average win/loss  What a winning trade makes on average, and what a
                    losing one costs. The pair beside win rate is what
                    tells you if the system makes money.
  Expectancy        What one trade is worth on average, in dollars:
                    (win% × avg win) − (loss% × avg loss). Above zero,
                    the system makes money over time. Below zero, it
                    loses, no matter the win rate.
  R                 One "R" is the amount risked on a trade (our 1%).
                    "+2R" means the trade made twice what it risked.
  Profit factor     Total money won ÷ total money lost. 1.0 is break-
                    even. 1.3+ is respectable. 3+ is usually a bug.
  Drawdown          How far the account fell from its highest point,
                    in percent. 8% is our line; 5% halves the size.
  Sharpe            Return per unit of wobble. Above 1 is good for a
                    simple system. Above 3 is usually a bug.
  n                 How many trades the number is built on. "81% (n=13)"
                    means thirteen trades — not enough to trust yet.
  Regime            What kind of market it is right now: calm / normal
                    / elevated / storm, and uptrend / range / downtrend.
                    Every strategy has regimes it is not allowed in.
  Gate (G0–G6)      How far a strategy has been proven: written down →
                    backtested → walked forward → stress-tested → shadow
                    → paper trading → seasoned. Nothing is "proven" at
                    G3; G6 is ninety trading days.
  Backtest          Running the rules on old prices to see what would
                    have happened. Easy to cheat by accident; section 14
                    is the list of ways we refuse to.
  Walk-forward      Testing on data the rules never saw when their
                    settings were chosen, in rolling slices. Our verdict
                    is the WORST slice.
  Monte Carlo       Shuffling the order of trades thousands of times to
                    see how bad a run of luck could get.
  Slippage          The difference between the price you wanted and the
                    price you got. We charge it on every simulated fill.
  Stop              A price at which the trade is closed automatically
                    to cap the loss.
  ATR               Average true range — how much the price typically
                    moves in a day. Stops are set in ATRs.
  Delta             For an option, roughly the chance it ends up in the
                    money. A 15-delta put is about a 15% chance.
  DTE               Days to expiry. We sell 30–45 days out and exit by
                    21.
  IV rank           Whether options are expensive right now compared to
                    the last year. We sell premium when it is ≥ 20.
  Credit spread     Sell one option, buy a cheaper one further out. You
                    keep the difference if the market behaves; your
                    worst case is fixed the moment you enter.
  Iron condor       A credit spread on both sides at once. Wins when the
                    market goes nowhere.
  Lane              One councillor's job: one family of strategies, one
                    strategy at a time.
  Shadow            The strategy runs and logs what it WOULD do, with no
                    orders. Ten clean days of that before it trades.
  Heat              How much of the whole council's money is at risk
                    right now across all open trades. Capped at 4%.
  PDT               A US rule: four day-trades in five days under $25k
                    locks the account. Applies to paper too. We do not
                    day-trade equities.
  IEX feed          The free price feed our paper account sees; close to
                    the real market, not identical. Disclosed.
  Money board       One line per business idea: real dollars in, real
                    dollars out, hours, and the next action. Zero until
                    a payment is confirmed.


=====================================================================
26. WHAT "SMARTER" WILL LOOK LIKE ON DAY 21, IN ONE PARAGRAPH
=====================================================================

I say "Odin, how's the hall." He says the regime in one line, five lanes
with a strategy, a gate and a scorecard each, every win rate with its n
and its expectancy beside it, the council's drawdown from peak, and
"paper, simulated." I ask why Volstagg did nothing and he reads me the
rule that stopped him. On Sunday he tells me which strategy the
tournament retired and which one replaced it, and why in one sentence,
and lists the proposals waiting for my approval. Frigga's lane, if it is
doing what the long history says it does, shows a win rate in the
seventies or low eighties beside a positive expectancy and a profit
factor over 1.2, on however many trades it has actually closed — and if
it is not doing that, he says that instead. The money board shows real
dollars or zeros, never a guess. None of it can touch real money. That is
smarter. Build exactly that.

Start now. Read the repo and every doc in docs/, then Day 1.
